# Audio research and review notes

Research date: 2026-11-12. No audio was listened to. Selection used the source's species/call
identification, spectrograms, waveform envelopes, and decoded measurements. This is a bounded
search of Commons, NPS/FWS and publicly readable Freesound pages, not proof that an unavailable
recording does not exist anywhere. No animal calls were synthesized. No footsteps were synthesized.

The final inventory and per-file licence/cut records are in `sources.json` and
[`assets/audio/SOURCES.md`](../../assets/audio/SOURCES.md). The measured format, peak, loudness,
and loop-boundary table is in [VERIFICATION.md](VERIFICATION.md).

## Names intentionally not delivered

| Name | Reason |
|---|---|
| `tiger_chuff` | A genuine CC0 tiger growl/snarl recording was found, but no source-identified chuff suitable for a confident cut was found. A growl was not relabelled as a chuff. |
| `wolf_growl` | Verified PD wolf howls were found; no licensed, source-identified wolf growl was located in the searched sources. |
| `polar_bear_growl` | No recording with verifiable field-recording provenance was located. A Commons CC0 item only describes itself as a sound effect; the readable Freesound hits were human voices or designed monsters. |
| `polar_bear_chuff` | Same provenance problem as above; no verified polar-bear chuff was located. |
| `elephant_trumpet` | **Delivered as a labelled species substitute (lead's decision, 2026-11-13; see below).** No identified, reusable trumpet of an African elephant (`Loxodonta africana`) was found: other hits were human/instrument imitations, AI generation, or unidentified elephant species. |
| `meerkat_growl` | Verified meerkat alarm chirps were found, but a distinct growl was not identified. |
| `ostrich_grunt` | Commons scientific-name/audio searches and Freesound searches found pronunciations, generic pack descriptions, or references to other archives without a verified reusable adult grunt recording. |
| `ostrich_hiss` | No individual adult ostrich hiss file with a verified acceptable licence and downloadable recording was located in those searches. |
| `kangaroo_grunt` | Searches for kangaroo / `Osphranter rufus` yielded speech, music, or unrelated effects, not a verified red-kangaroo recording. |

## Substitutions and ShareAlike exceptions

- `elephant_trumpet.ogg`: a real elephant trumpet, but of an **Asian** elephant
  (`Elephas maximus`; Commons `File:Elephant voice - trumpeting.ogg`, the uploader's own
  recording, CC0, SHA-1 on Commons `1bf7722d…` matched the download). The zoo keeps African
  bush elephants. Both research rounds withheld the file as the wrong species; the lead took it
  on 2026-11-13 because the game otherwise has no trumpet at all, and labelled it a species
  substitute everywhere it is recorded (`sources.json`, `assets/audio/SOURCES.md`,
  `assets/ATTRIBUTION.md`). It must not be described as an African-elephant recording, and it
  is to be replaced as soon as an identified `Loxodonta africana` trumpet with a usable licence
  turns up. Nobody listened to it; its waveform is one burst of about 0.9 s.
- `lion_growl.ogg` and `_2`: low grunts from the end of a species-identified lion roaring bout.
- `zebra_whinny.ogg`: a plains-zebra bray, explicitly described by the recordist as distinct
  from horse neighing; the same-species bray is the stand-in for this requested name.
- `penguin_bray.ogg`: a short African-penguin call from the supplementary recording. It is
  not an entire multi-syllable ecstatic-display song; exact call subtype remains unverified.
- `meerkat_chirp.ogg`, `_2`, `_3`: **CC BY-SA 3.0 fallback**. Commons `Suricata`/meerkat
  searches and Freesound searches found no verified CC0/PD/CC BY recording of meerkat calls.
  Human vocalizations, pronunciation files, and calls of other mongoose species were excluded.
  These three edited recordings remain CC BY-SA 3.0.
- `camel_grunt.ogg`, `_2`: **CC BY-SA 3.0 fallback**. The Commons description (including
  Russian identification) and category identify Bactrian camel. The CC BY Freesound candidate
  `camel.flac` does not identify a camel species; an unanswered comment asks that question.
  No species-verified CC0/PD/CC BY Bactrian-camel call was found. Both edited recordings remain
  CC BY-SA 3.0, with the complete author list supplied by Commons preserved.

The repository does not change the licences of edited recordings to MIT. The MIT statement
covers repository scripts and records. See the linked CC licence for each recording's terms.

## Credit and licence discrepancies resolved

- The Commons grizzly MP3 has an `Artist` field naming Jennifer Jerrett, but its description
  says Dan Bergum recorded the bear. The [original NPS item](https://www.nps.gov/media/video/view.htm?id=48C181D5-1DD8-B71B-0BE5FF0F921CAA4F)
  credits **NPS/Dan Bergum** and explicitly explains public-domain use for NPS-credited media.
  The delivered records credit Dan Bergum and preserve the discrepancy. Cut times refer to the
  downloaded 65.135417 s Commons MP3, not the longer original NPS item.
- The Commons elephant-rumble Ogg is tagged **CC BY 2.5**, outside this brief's allowed versions.
  It was not downloaded or used. The [current publisher PLOS Figshare record](https://plos.figshare.com/articles/dataset/Bee_Threat_Elicits_Alarm_Call_in_African_Elephants/143732)
  and its [own API metadata](https://api.figshare.com/v2/articles/143732) explicitly identify
  **CC BY 4.0** and list original `Audio_S3.wav` (file ID 424086). That original WAV, its source
  checksum, the publisher's licence object, and the listed authors are the delivered source.
- The Commons giraffe **hum** derivative has CC BY-SA 4.0 metadata. It was not used. The
  [original publisher's article](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s13104-015-1394-3)
  licenses the work CC BY 4.0 and supplies a separate, explicitly identified **grunt** in
  Additional file 2. The library uses that original MOV's audio, not the Commons derivative.

## Measurement-based review and uncertainties

Source spectrograms were generated with `showspectrumpic` and inspected in the tool viewer for:
PD lion, the CC BY lion bout, tiger, FWS wolves, grizzly, giraffe, penguin, meerkat, greater
flamingo, white rhino, camel and elephant. The elephant excerpt preserves the low-frequency
band. Lion bouts show repeated low-frequency pulses; wolf howls show sustained harmonic
bands; the giraffe source has a brief low grunt near 0.9 s. Meerkat cuts select distinct warning
pulses from the first half of the source, avoiding its apparent repeated second half.
Footstep waveforms were inspected; the very quiet paved and grass sources were also viewed
with display gain. The gravel file explicitly separates five walking takes from five running
takes, and only the walking portion is used. All final files also have 100 ms `astats` RMS
envelopes; none of the cuts is all silence.

No measurement can prove the absence of intelligible words or establish an animal call's
subtype. **Every recording remains unlistened-to.** The following files have additional
uncertainty requiring particular attention during the lead's listening review:

| Files | Specific uncertainty |
|---|---|
| `animals/tiger_growl.ogg`, `_2`, `_3` | Source explicitly warns of strong enclosure reverberation; spectrograms cannot rule out quiet background visitors. |
| `animals/brown_bear_growl.ogg` | Weak warning vocalization assigned to growl from the source's mixed huff/growl description; precise call type is uncertain. |
| `animals/brown_bear_huff.ogg`, `_2` | Mixed huffing/jaw-popping recording during capture; precise breath-versus-jaw-pop content needs listening. |
| `animals/rhino_grunt.ogg`, `animals/rhino_snort.ogg`, `_2` | Source identifies white rhinos fighting in a waterhole; exact grunt/snort classification and separation from splashes remain provisional. |
| `animals/penguin_bray.ogg` | Short call substituted for full bray; exact vocal subtype unverified. |
| `animals/camel_grunt.ogg`, `_2` | Recording is identified as Bactrian camel by Commons, but the original contributor list does not assign one individual recordist. Growl/grunt nomenclature is approximate. |
| `ambience/crowd.ogg` | Source describes indistinct open-air conversation; no foreground sentence or music can be excluded by spectrogram alone. |
| `ambience/park_day.ogg` | Source states no traffic; the absence of very distant human/urban sounds needs listening. |
| `footsteps/paved_1.ogg` through `_5`, `footsteps/grass_1.ogg` through `_5` | Sources are quiet; substantial gain exposes background texture. Cuts contain one dominant impact, but shoe/scuff realism needs listening. |

The intended compromises are recorded rather than hidden behind generic animal names. No C++
API or source file was changed. Missing names should be handled as absent optional assets;
adding a later recording needs a new per-file source/licence check.

Additional assumptions: walking shoe/boot source descriptions were treated as adult steps;
recordist ages are not documented. Call-type substitutions above are permitted same-species
substitutions, not claims that each requested label is scientifically exact. Early discovery
included an overlapping search and metadata lookup and received HTTP 429; acquisition was then
serialized with five-second spacing and longer HTTP-error backoff. No further parallel network
fetches were used. No source licence was inferred from a site's general reputation or from a
similarly named file.

## Round 2 — 2026-11-13

Research uses serialized requests with User-Agent `zoo-simulator-audio-fetch/1.0`,
five-second pauses, cached responses, and a 300,000,000-byte cumulative response budget
(including metadata). Raw responses and the URL/status/byte ledger are in
`build-probe/audio-src/round2-requests.json`. An HTTP failure is an access limitation,
not evidence that a collection has no recordings. No recording has been listened to.

The required source families are investigated for all seven taxa (nine call names).
Xeno-canto applies to the two ostrich names only; FWS/NOAA/USGS applies to the two
polar-bear names only. A species-level query covers both requested calls where applicable.

### Initial candidate review

- iNaturalist observation [48825579](https://www.inaturalist.org/observations/48825579),
  sound 103908, Tuoichen: research-grade *Loxodonta africana*, Samburu, CC BY.
  GBIF occurrence 2641525056 independently supplies the sound-specific CC BY 4.0 URL.
  The observation page returned HTTP 403; both API records remain cached.
  The downloaded 35-second audio was inspected as a waveform and log-frequency spectrogram
  (`round2-elephant-inspection.png`). There are strong changing harmonic/formant bands
  around 0–2, 3–5 and 9–12 seconds, low-frequency background and abrupt joins.
  The observation describes elephant activities but does not identify a trumpet or map
  sounds to events. Those features alone cannot exclude foreground speech or establish
  a trumpet; this candidate is withheld, not promoted to an authentic trumpet.
- GBIF tiger results include Borror recordings with proprietary copyright and
  Tierstimmenarchiv recordings under CC BY-NC-SA 4.0. The seven polar-bear media
  and four meerkat media are also CC BY-NC-SA 4.0. None is usable under this brief.
- GBIF ostrich occurrence 1142105508 identifies *Struthio camelus* and one
  Tierstimmenarchiv sound with CC BY-SA 4.0. Its original record and sound are
  being checked; other returned ostrich sounds carry NC and/or ND restrictions.

Broad Zenodo species queries return many taxonomy images, papers and genetic tables.
Focused call queries are therefore required before treating this family as searched.
Dryad compound queries are supplemented with simple call phrases, avoiding reliance
on undocumented Boolean-search semantics. Internet Archive broad queries mostly return
music, audiobooks and podcasts; focused call queries follow. CD rips and falsely
PD-labelled commercial entertainment are excluded regardless of their licence field.

The ostrich Tierstimmenarchiv candidate was rejected after opening its own record:
[Struthio_camelus_Lue_43_4_0](https://www.tierstimmenarchiv.de/webinterface/contents/showdetails.php?edit=-1&unique_id=TSA:Struthio_camelus_Lue_43_4_0&autologin=true)
says **“Flügelschütteln.”** and classifies the sound as **“wing sound”**. It is not
a vocalization. The stale GBIF media download URL also returned 404; the current page
links a new download endpoint, which need not be fetched for this rejected candidate.

Commons combined `(filetype:audio OR filetype:video)` queries returned zero results
even where a simple `Loxodonta africana filetype:audio` query returned five. Those
combined-query zeros are not used as negative evidence; separate audio and video
queries replace them. Zenodo focused queries with `size=50` returned HTTP 400 and
are repeated with simple phrases and `size=25`; failures are not treated as empty searches.

Further exclusions checked in Round 2:

- [Zenodo 7831997](https://zenodo.org/records/7831997), *Directional speakers as a tool
  for animal vocal communication studies*, explicitly identifies *Suricata suricatta*
  and CC BY 4.0, but supplies a recruitment-call playback track and speaker-comparison
  recordings. The authors warn that the parametric speaker strongly changes call structure
  and does not reliably reproduce low frequencies. Neither “recruitment” nor a distorted
  speaker playback is promoted to a source-identified growl. Large experimental videos
  were not downloaded. The individual WAV file listings and licence remain in the API cache.
- xeno-canto's old unauthenticated API returned 404; the species page is accessible.
  Adult boom recordings XC845940 and XC845942 are CC BY-NC-SA 4.0; recordings
  XC1080573–XC1080577 and XC834114/XC834116 are CC BY-NC-ND 4.0.
  XC46725 (CC BY-NC-ND 2.5) explicitly describes an **African Rock Python hissing
  in foreground**, and cannot supply `ostrich_hiss` irrespective of its licence.
- Internet Archive `SSE_Library_ANIMALS` and `Designers-Choice-Collection-Animals`
  claim CC0 but are edited/designed effects collections without the required species,
  recordist and place evidence for these calls. `Arianne-Project_Stendhal-Sound-Effects`
  is a game asset extraction and is excluded. `PupDistress2.wav` says meerkat pup
  distress call, not growl, and the search record does not establish recordist/place.

Dryad file-level checks (the API file listings, not just paper titles, were inspected):
versions 374863 (elephant population rumbles), 370902 (wolf larynx), 3509
(vocal repertoire), 309216 (predator-call experiment), and meerkat versions
18482, 26683, 5734, 245197, 231113 and 90766 expose tables, measured acoustic
features, scripts or anatomical meshes, not reusable call audio. Version 3509
is specifically a chacma-baboon table, not wolf audio. The new elephant dataset
version 464258 does expose `clips.zip` (120,802,626 bytes), `audio.zip`
(3,730,741,306 bytes), and small label/README files. The full audio archive is
excluded by the task's download budget; the small metadata is inspected first.

### Downloaded Part 1 media inspection decisions

No cuts were made from these candidates. Waveforms and logarithmic-frequency
spectrograms are cached as `round2-candidate-231/234/235/236-inspection.png`.

| Candidate | Evidence and decision |
|---|---|
| Commons `Rallying.ogg` | PD, source identifies European wolves rallying. The 18.12 s waveform is continuously active, with overlapping sustained modulated harmonic bands, not an isolated source-identified growl. No confident growl cut. |
| `Cub polar bear is nursing 2.ogv` | CC BY-SA 3.0; species/context identified. FFprobe finds video only, **no audio stream**. |
| `Play fight of polar bears.wmv.ogv` | CC BY-SA 3.0; Churchill polar bears. FFprobe finds video only, **no audio stream**. |
| `Erdmaennchen leipzig raufen.ogv` | CC BY-SA 3.0, own recording of meerkats tussling. Its 19 s audio has strong changing harmonic events against continuous background. Source does not label a growl; visual evidence cannot distinguish animal calls from visitor speech or establish an isolated foreground growl. Withheld. |
| `Ostrich mating.webm` | CC BY-SA 4.0, own recording along an Arizona freeway. About 21 s of nearly continuous broadband and low-frequency noise; no isolated, clearly dominant boom/hiss established. Withheld. |
| `Struisvogel paringsdans libtheora.ogg` | CC BY-SA 4.0, own courtship video. 69.03 s; audio decodes to **6,088,798 zero-valued channel samples**, maximum absolute sample 0. No sound to cut. |
| `Sumatran tiger (Panthera tigris sumatrae) vocalising.webm` | Original Flickr credit identifies yowling/meowing, not chuffing; CC BY **2.0**, outside permitted BY versions. Not downloaded. |

Both xeno-canto species pages were inspected: all 44 listed recordings have NC
and/or ND restrictions. A boom would be the natural same-species interpretation
of `ostrich_grunt`, but none of these booms can be used under the allowed licences.

Federal searches: the legacy FWS National Digital Library API redirects to the
current FWS image-search page, not sound records. Current FWS general/video search
pages did not establish an individually licensed polar-bear call. NOAA main search
returned 403; USGS search returned 504, then timed out on retry. Bing RSS fallback
queries returned irrelevant “Polar” brands and television results even with the
federal site filters; these are explicitly **not** negative evidence about the
federal collections. Access limitations are retained, not reported as zero hits.

### Part 1 outcome

All nine names remain absent. This is a bounded, documented search, not a claim
that authentic recordings do not exist. iNaturalist, GBIF, Zenodo, Dryad, Internet
Archive and multilingual/scientific-name Commons audio, video and category searches
were made for each taxon; ostrich additionally had both xeno-canto pages checked,
and polar bear had FWS, NOAA and USGS attempts with the access limits above.

| Name | Final reason / most relevant findings |
|---|---|
| `elephant_trumpet` | iNaturalist's licensed Loxodonta africana file has no confirmed trumpet; Commons trumpet is Elephas maximus. Dryad's public dataset README explicitly identifies all 1,123 clips as manually identified rumbles (0.2 s buffers), so the 121 MB archive was not downloaded for a trumpet. Zenodo/Archive did not establish an eligible trumpet. |
| `tiger_chuff` | GBIF recordings are NC/proprietary; Commons has growls and a BY 2.0 yowling video, not an eligible chuff. Dryad/Zenodo chuff/prusten searches and Archive found no verified chuff file. |
| `wolf_growl` | iNaturalist/GBIF contain howls, mixed or unspecified calls and domestic dogs. PD Commons rallying has sustained overlapping harmonic calls, no confident isolated growl. Dryad/Zenodo/Archive do not establish an eligible growl. |
| `polar_bear_growl` | GBIF media are NC-SA; Commons play-fight videos checked have no audio. Generic sound effect lacks authentic-recording provenance. No suitable dataset/Archive/federal item verified. |
| `polar_bear_chuff` | Same searches as growl, including cub/nursing terms; nursing video has no audio. No source-identified chuff verified. Federal access limitations remain explicit. |
| `meerkat_growl` | No eligible iNaturalist observations; GBIF is NC-SA. Zenodo recruitment/speaker tracks and Archive pup distress are not identified growls. Commons tussling audio cannot establish an isolated growl. |
| `ostrich_grunt` | GBIF SA candidate is wing shaking; all 44 xeno-canto entries have NC/ND restrictions, including the explicit booms. One Commons courtship video is digitally silent; another is dominated by continuous noise. No eligible boom established in datasets/Archive. |
| `ostrich_hiss` | Same searches, plus hiss queries. XC46725 identifies the foreground hiss as a python; no eligible ostrich hiss established. |
| `kangaroo_grunt` | No eligible Osphranter rufus/Macropus rufus sound observations or GBIF sound media. Zenodo/Dryad cough/cluck/vocalization queries do not provide red-kangaroo calls. Both Commons red-kangaroo videos inspected have no audio stream (8.008 s original; 36.036 s small derivative of the larger original). |

The Dryad public page resolves its API-download obstacle for the purpose of
classification: the README is embedded in the HTML and identifies the clips as
rumbles; no trumpet archive download is warranted. NOAA Fisheries' corrected
`oq` search returns marine-mammal research/viewing/publication results, not a
source-identified polar-bear call. FWS's current results rely on a client-rendered
search and did not expose a usable individual audio record in the fetched page.
No federal licence was assumed from an agency logo or from a general homepage.
### Part 2 — original passages inspected first

Full-source waveform/spectrograms were generated and viewed for all six unique
sources behind the seven single-file names (`round2-original-*-inspection.png`).
The original elephant WAV is only 6.407528 s and its existing 0.95–5.55 s cut
already occupies the useful rumble. The giraffe MOV is 1.985 s with one brief grunt;
the penguin Oga is 1.580408 s with one isolated short call; the zebra MP3 is
3.408 s and its bark/whinny cuts already cover the braying bout. None can supply
additional non-overlapping full calls without reusing a call or changing the
meaning of “take”. The 65.135417 s bear and 91.032 s rhino sources have further
transients, but their precise call types are not identified by time in the source;
the only clearly sustained rhino low call is already in the grunt cut. Additional
huffs/snorts/splashes were not relabelled as growls/grunts. Alternative sources are
therefore searched next. Existing A1 call-type uncertainties remain documented.

### Round 2 Part 2 — alternative takes and decisions

All seven single-take names were checked in the original recordings first, then the
species-filtered iNaturalist, Commons audio and Zenodo queries in the ledger. No repeated
or overlapping cuts were counted as new takes.

| Name | Alternative-source decision |
|---|---|
| `brown_bear_growl` | iNaturalist: no eligible sounds. Commons alternatives are explicitly roars or feeding sounds, not confidently another growl. Zenodo exact brown-bear/growl query found none. Original unused huffs/jaw pops are not relabelled. Remains one take. |
| `elephant_rumble` | Added original PLOS/Figshare Audio S1 (natural bee-response rumble) and Movie S3 (original-speed oral rumble). Both records explicitly identify Loxodonta africana and CC BY 4.0. Rejected Audio S2's experimentally changed formants and slow-motion Movies S2/S4. Original source Audio S3 stays take 1. |
| `giraffe_grunt` | No eligible iNaturalist sounds or exact Zenodo grunt result. Commons grunt is the same A1 recording; other files are snorts, bursts and hums. A1's single short grunt cannot supply independent cuts. Remains one take. |
| `penguin_bray` | PLOS 2014 Video S3 explicitly identifies an ecstatic display song; Nature 2015 Supplementary Video S1 identifies another display song. Both identify Spheniscus demersus and CC BY 4.0. Their inspected spectrograms contain distinct sequences (one versus three long syllables). These become takes 1 and 3. A1's short contact call remains take 2, explicitly labelled as a contact-call variant rather than a bray. |
| `rhino_grunt` | No eligible iNaturalist sound; Commons audio result is pronunciation, not an animal. Zenodo individual-distinctiveness record exposes no audio file. Original source's other clear short events are snorts, not confidently grunts. Remains one take. |
| `zebra_bark`, `zebra_whinny` | iNaturalist 293825875 (Equus quagga; Christine Stone; sound 1547380 CC0) has very weak waveform, continuous background and no distinct foreground call in the inspected spectrogram; rejected. Commons results do not establish an additional identified bark/whinny. Zenodo 12200413 is CC0 and has an audio subfolder, but only inside a 184,622,687-byte ZIP; its API offers whole archives, not individual audio members. After the accepted small publisher files, that ZIP would exceed the 300 MB cumulative download budget. Its README and full metadata were inspected; the archive was not downloaded. Both names remain one take. |

The iNaturalist African-penguin observation 378392423 (Ryan van Huyssteen; sound
2057973, `cc-by`) has a dominant display-shaped pulse/long-syllable sequence in its
waveform/spectrogram, but no source call label and its item webpage returns HTTP 403.
The publisher-labelled display recordings have stronger evidence and were selected instead.
The iNaturalist candidate was downloaded for inspection, not used or assigned a guessed
licence version. Zenodo penguin results concern at-sea calls or restricted begging datasets,
not a public bray recording.

Accepted source inspections: `round2-elephant-bee-inspection.png` (continuous low rumble
and higher formants, 0.13–4.39 s selected); `round2-elephant-oral-inspection.png`
(rising/falling low harmonic band, 0.30–2.62 s selected); `round2-penguin-display-inspection.png`
(short pulses, long syllable, alternating ending, 6.00–11.85 s selected);
`round2-penguin-display-second-inspection.png` (three long syllables with intervening
short syllables, 3.40–9.40 s selected). Penguin selections are clearly described as excerpts
of the longer display bouts. No overlapping selections, time stretching, pitch shifting,
noise reduction or synthetic animal sounds were used. The penguin 60 Hz high-pass matches
A1 and reduces low background below its vocal band; elephants retain their low frequencies.
Nobody listened to the files.
The final provenance cross-check of the elephant paper confirms that Audio S1 contains natural
bee-response rumbles used as playback stimuli, whereas S2 applies the experimental formant
change. The caption describes a three-rumble stimulus; the inspected example WAV does not
provide three confidently separable, non-overlapping complete takes. No additional takes
were invented from it. The accepted file remains described as an excerpt.
<!-- ROUND2-LEDGER-START -->
